| F5-S058 | Workspace sweep | `dev/active/ae03bcd0-general-bch/breakdown.json:5` |   "description": "Author the design document fixing the canonical relative finite-field extension abstraction for gf2-core and the stable algebraic field identity shared by serialization validation and automatic field selection.\n\n## Background\n\ngf2-core has concrete extension configurations (`ExtConfig` binomial towers, runtime `Gf2mField`) but no canonical public relationship expressing that one field extends another: no relative degree, checked membership, restriction, or relative Frobenius across two field types. Compile-time and runtime quotient fields will coexist, so the design must fix one identity and conversion semantics between them. Field identity is algebraic — characteristic, base tower, modulus, basis semantics — and is distinct from the element wire representation, which serialized headers version separately.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n- [hard] REQ-01: A design document in the epic's dev/active directory defines the extension trait surface (embedding, checked membership and restriction, relative degree, relative Frobenius, field-order relationships, validation certificates), citing the existing types it must cover.\n- [hard] REQ-02: The document fixes the algebraic field-identity representation, its distinctness from element wire representation, and the equivalence/conversion semantics between compile-time and runtime forms of the same field.\n- [hard] REQ-03: The document names which operations belong on the trait versus derived helpers, with a worked GF(2)/GF(2^m) and GF(p)/GF(p^r) example each, and preallocates the module layout for the foundation tasks that follow.\n", | Outside protocol scope: separate family/domain work at `dev/active/ae03bcd0-general-bch/breakdown.json:5`; shared-runner fix: `dev/tools/tuning-campaign-support/src/abtest.rs:349`. |

| F5-S061 | Workspace sweep | `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:129` |     "description": "A production sampler that draws uniform random matrices over $\\mathbb{F}_q$ for $q \\in \\{3,5,7\\}$ from a reproducible address space whose purposes and streams are injective by construction, hosted in a new narrow workspace crate.\n\n## Background\n\nA published sampling dataset must let any shard be regenerated from its address alone, so the random-matrix source has to be version-stable and partitioned by construction rather than by audit. Two in-tree generators fall short of that. `gf2_algebra::testutil::random_matrix` reduces an MMIX LCG output modulo $q$ and is a test helper. `FieldMatrix::random_seeded` (`crates/gf2-core/src/field/matrix.rs:617`) takes a single `u64` seed, draws through `StdRng` — whose algorithm carries no cross-version reproducibility guarantee, so a dataset seeded through it stops being regenerable after a dependency bump — offers no partition of the stream space, and produces `FieldMatrix<Fp<P>>`. The gap is therefore not the absence of a uniform sampler but the absence of one with domain-separated, reproducible stream addressing.\n\nRejection sampling is required rather than optional. The sampler consumes bytes and $256 \\bmod 7 = 4$, so reducing a byte without rejection would give four residues probability $37/256$ and three of them $36/256$ against a uniform $1/7$. In relative terms the worst class is off by $1.56\\%$, about $22$ times the $0.07\\%$ relative resolution a standard error of $10^{-4}$ on $\\Pr[\\mathrm{per}(A)=0] \\approx 1/7$ represents.\n\nA prototype carrying these properties, with entry-uniformity and stream-separation tests, is `dev/research/permanent-sampling-feas/src/sampler.rs`; it is the reference to lift from.\n\nThe ground this module stands on is laid by prerequisite leaves: `rand_chacha` and `rand_core` 0.9 are declared once at workspace level, and the `gf2-stats` member crate exists with `gf2-core` as its only in-workspace dependency. This leaf registers the sampler module in that crate and implements it.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n- [hard] REQ-01: Matrix draws use ChaCha20 seeded from a 32-byte block of four little-endian `u64` words: campaign root, $q$, $n$, and a fourth word whose top $8$ bits hold the purpose tag and whose low $56$ bits hold the stream index.\n- [hard] REQ-02: Seed derivation asserts the stream index is below $2^{56}$ and refuses to derive otherwise, so the map from $(\\text{root}, q, n, \\text{purpose}, \\text{stream})$ to seed block is injective by construction rather than by convention.\n- [hard] REQ-03: Entries are exactly uniform over $\\mathbb{F}_q$: a test draws at least $3 \\times 10^5$ entries per field for $q \\in \\{3,5,7\\}$ and finds each residue class within six binomial standard errors of $1/q$.\n- [hard] REQ-04: Address separation is asserted at the address level: a test over a set of addresses differing in each component in turn finds every derived seed block distinct, and a further test finds two generators started from distinct addresses diverge in their raw output stream.\n- [hard] REQ-05: Committed golden vectors pin, for a fixed set of addresses spanning each field and each purpose tag, the derived 32-byte seed block and the first drawn matrix, and a test compares against them.\n- [hard] REQ-06: Two draws from one address tuple return identical matrices, demonstrated by test.\n- [hard] REQ-07: The draw-to-entry mapping is documented and tested as $A[k / n][k \\bmod n]$ row-major for draw index $k$, before any packed constructor reorders it into kernel storage.\n- [hard] REQ-08: A draw fills a caller-supplied row-major entry buffer without a per-draw heap allocation, so a caller can pack directly into the permanent kernels' matrix types.\n\n## Notes\n\nStream separation is a statement about addresses and generator state, not about output values: two disjoint streams may legitimately draw the same small matrix, and at $q = 3$, $n = 2$ there are only $81$ matrices in total. Tests therefore assert distinct derived seeds, distinct generator output, and golden-vector agreement, never that two streams share no matrix.\n\nThe crate carries no dependency on the algebra layer: packing into kernel matrix types is the caller's concern, which keeps the sampler usable from validation harnesses that never touch a permanent kernel.", | Outside protocol scope: separate family/domain work at `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:129`; shared-runner fix: `dev/tools/tuning-campaign-support/src/abtest.rs:349`. |

| F5-S064 | Workspace sweep | `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:231` |     "description": "The conservative small-count interval: a Clopper-Pearson estimator validated against an independent inversion of its own definition, with empirical coverage demonstrated in the regime the rectangular validation lives in.\n\n## Background\n\nNo Clopper-Pearson estimator exists anywhere in-tree, and the rectangular validation cells — where the estimated event probability is of order $q^{-n}$ and counts are in the tens — are exactly where a normal-approximation interval's coverage is weakest. Clopper-Pearson is conservative by construction and keeps its nominal coverage at counts in the tens.\n\nThe two estimators answer different questions. Checking one against the other proves nothing — containment follows from how they are built — so this estimator is validated against an independent computation of its own definition, and its coverage is demonstrated empirically rather than assumed from construction.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n- [hard] REQ-01: A Clopper-Pearson interval is returned for a count out of $N$ at a caller-supplied level and matches, to a stated tolerance, an independent computation of its own bounds by inversion of the binomial tail or equivalently by beta quantiles, on cases spanning $Z = 0$, $Z = N$, and counts in the tens.\n- [hard] REQ-02: Coverage is demonstrated empirically: over repeated simulated draws at stated $(N, p)$ including small $N$ and small $p$, the fraction of intervals containing $p$ is at least the nominal level.\n- [hard] REQ-03: The estimator returns a well-formed interval at $N = 2 \\times 10^{7}$, with no overflow, no interval of negative width, and no bound outside $[0,1]$.\n- [hard] REQ-04: Documentation states which estimator suits which regime, so a caller at counts in the tens is not left with the normal-approximation interval by default.\n\n## Notes\n\nEntry points take counts, not samples, matching the Wilson estimator beside it, so both are usable online against a running accumulator and offline against a published dataset.\n", | Algebraic binomial or independent event/frame model; no decoded-bit estimator at `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:231`. |

| F5-S066 | Workspace sweep | `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:255` |     "description": "The two exact binomial tests the campaign's acceptance decisions rest on, with a result interface that stays correct where an exact tail underflows double precision.\n\n## Background\n\nThe campaign's acceptance decisions rest on two nulls known exactly rather than approximately. The permanent floor $\\Pr[\\mathrm{per}(A) = 0] \\ge 1/q$ is a composite null whose worst case is $p = 1/q$, so a test evaluated there is valid against the whole null. The determinant companion is pinned at the exact finite-$n$ value $1 - \\prod_{i=1}^{n}(1 - q^{-i})$. Both are binomial observations of a count out of a fixed $N$, so both admit an exact $p$-value; the normal-approximation critical values that appear in planning arithmetic ($z = 3.16$ one-sided, $z = 3.36$ two-sided at a family of $63$ cells) are sizing tools, not decision rules.\n\nExactness has a numerical consequence the interface must face rather than hide. At $N = 2 \\times 10^{7}$ a preregistered per-cell level of order $10^{-4}$ sits far out in the distribution, and the exact tail sum legitimately underflows double precision. An interface returning a plain probability silently reports zero there, which is indistinguishable from a catastrophic disagreement and equally indistinguishable from a bug.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n- [hard] REQ-01: A one-sided exact binomial test of the composite null $\\Pr[\\mathrm{per}(A) = 0] \\ge 1/q$ evaluates the null at its worst case $p = 1/q$ and returns a result from the binomial distribution, matching a brute-force sum over the probability mass function for $N \\le 200$.\n- [hard] REQ-02: A two-sided exact binomial test against a caller-supplied exact probability returns a result under a stated and documented two-sided convention, matching a brute-force sum for $N \\le 200$.\n- [hard] REQ-03: The result interface reports on a log scale, or as a direct comparison against a caller-supplied threshold, so a decision stays correct where the exact tail underflows double precision.\n- [hard] REQ-04: A test at $N = 2 \\times 10^{7}$ with a preregistered per-cell level returns a decision and a finite log-scale statistic rather than a probability that has flushed to zero, for counts on both sides of the threshold.\n- [hard] REQ-05: A level-allocation helper divides a caller-supplied error budget across a caller-supplied number of tests and returns the per-test level, documented so one global budget can be split across more than one test family.\n- [hard] REQ-06: Documentation states that normal-approximation critical values are planning approximations for sizing, and that acceptance decisions use these exact tests.\n\n## Notes\n\nThe tests take counts and a null value, so the same entry points serve a running campaign and a later reanalysis of a published dataset.", | Outside protocol scope: separate family/domain work at `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:255`; shared-runner fix: `dev/tools/tuning-campaign-support/src/abtest.rs:349`. |
| F5-S067 | Workspace sweep | `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:840` |     "description": "Per-cell acceptance tests wired into the campaign driver: an exact test of the proved permanent floor, an exact two-sided test of the determinant companion, one global error budget across both families, and a halt-and-preserve rule with a predeclared retry policy.\n\n## Background\n\nThe floor $\\Pr[\\mathrm{per}(A) = 0] \\ge 1/q$ is proved for every $n$ at odd characteristic, so a cell significantly below $1/q$ indicts the pipeline rather than the mathematics, and such a cell can never be reported as a scientific finding. Running that check on every cell costs nothing and is sharper than small-$n$ enumeration anchors, which say nothing at the sizes a campaign cares about.\n\nIts false-alarm rate has to be controlled or the check destroys itself. A nominal $95\\%$ interval misses its parameter $5\\%$ of the time and only the lower excursion trips a one-sided test, so a single cell sitting at $1/q$ — the worst case the theorem allows — flags with probability up to $2.5\\%$; across a grid of $63$ cells that compounds to a $79\\%$ chance of at least one false alarm, halting a healthy campaign nearly every run. The correction is an explicit family-wise error rate, and it must be allocated across both test families together rather than spending a fresh budget on each.\n\nThe determinant companion is checked against the exact finite-$n$ singular probability, never against its $n \\to \\infty$ limit. The limit overstates the exact value by $3.5 \\times 10^{-3}$ at $q=3$, $n=4$ and by $3.8 \\times 10^{-4}$ at $q=3$, $n=6$ — tens to hundreds of standard errors at cells that cost minutes to sample — so a limit-based check would manufacture failures exactly where the campaign's data is tightest.\n\nRerunning a halted cell until it passes spends the error budget silently, so retry behaviour is predeclared rather than decided in the moment.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n- [hard] REQ-01: Each completed cell is checked against $\\Pr[\\mathrm{per}(A) = 0] \\ge 1/q$ by a one-sided exact binomial test of the composite null evaluated at its worst case $p = 1/q$.\n- [hard] REQ-02: Each completed cell for which the companion ran is checked by a two-sided exact binomial test of its determinant zero count against $1 - \\prod_{i=1}^{n}(1 - q^{-i})$ at that cell's $q$ and $n$, and a cell whose determinant state is not-evaluated is skipped rather than tested against a count it does not have.\n- [hard] REQ-03: The determinant reference value is computed per cell from the finite-$n$ product, and no code path compares a measured determinant fraction against an asymptotic limit.\n- [hard] REQ-04: Each checked completed cell's estimate and interval, for both the permanent and the determinant, are computed here and recorded with the cell's verdict, so estimation and decision share one code path.\n- [hard] REQ-05: The error budget is allocated across the permanent-floor family and the determinant family from one global total, with each family's share and each cell's resulting level fixed before any campaign matrix is drawn and recorded with the campaign.\n- [hard] REQ-06: A failing cell halts the campaign, and the failure is recorded with the cell identity, counts, test statistic and level, rather than being reported as a scientific finding.\n- [hard] REQ-07: A rerun after a halt is admitted only under the campaign's predeclared retry rule, which states what may be rerun, how many attempts are allowed, and how the error budget is charged; a rerun outside that rule is refused by the driver.\n- [hard] REQ-08: A rejecting completed cell's data and verdict are preserved in the dataset together with the contradiction. A halted cell preserves every valid manifested shard completed before the halt as pooled raw counts plus its reason, carries no final estimate or verdict, and a later run neither removes nor overwrites either record.\n- [hard] REQ-09: Acceptance decisions use exact tests only, taken through the log-scale or threshold-comparison interface so a decision stays correct where an exact tail underflows double precision; normal-approximation critical values appear nowhere in the decision path.\n- [hard] REQ-10: A test drives the acceptance layer with synthetic counts that must halt and with counts that must pass, asserting the verdict and the recorded evidence in both cases, including at a sample size where the exact tail underflows.\n\n## Notes\n\nThe proved upper bound $\\Pr \\le 1/q + 11/q^3$ is deliberately not part of the test: campaign cells sit tens to thousands of standard errors below it, so checking it would never fire and never inform.", | Outside protocol scope: separate family/domain work at `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:840`; shared-runner fix: `dev/tools/tuning-campaign-support/src/abtest.rs:349`. |

What finite data can address is the finite-$n$ correction $\\delta(n) = \\Pr[\\mathrm{per}(A) = 0] - 1/q$ — its sign, its magnitude, and how it varies with $n$ and $q$ — and the pipeline that measures it.\n\nThe proved results bound that quantity far too loosely to substitute for measurement. The floor $\\Pr \\ge 1/q$ holds for every $n$ at odd characteristic; the ceiling $\\Pr \\le 1/q + 11/q^3$ holds for $n \\ge 3$ but sits $0.03$ to $0.41$ above the floor, between one-and-a-half and three-and-a-half orders of magnitude looser than the deviations a campaign can resolve.\n\nPreregistration is what separates this campaign from the by-product statistics that preceded it. Those cells had no fixed sample size and no stopping rule, and one of them — a $q = 7$, $n = 20$ reading $2.30$ standard errors below the floor — is exactly the shape of result that a post-hoc threshold manufactures. An earlier revision of the feasibility study spent its top recommendation chasing an apparent below-floor signal that proved to be two measurement defects plus fluctuation.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n- [hard] REQ-01: The estimand is stated as $\\delta(n) = \\Pr[\\mathrm{per}(A) = 0] - 1/q$ over the measured range, with the explicit record that no finite grid tests an asymptotic statement in either the observed statistic.\n- [hard] REQ-03: The cell universe names a core processor-feasible grid — all $n$ from $4$ to the per-$q$ frontier, the frontier at a standard error of $10^{-3}$ being $n = 28$ at $q = 3$, $n = 24$ at $q = 5$ and $n = 20$ at $q = 7$, giving $K = 63$ cells — together with any predeclared accelerator-contingent extension cells.\n- [hard] REQ-04: Extension cells either enter the multiplicity accounting with the core grid or are placed in an explicitly gatekept second family whose entry condition is stated before any draw.\n- [hard] REQ-05: A global error budget is allocated numerically across the permanent-floor family and the determinant family, and the per-cell level each family uses follows from that allocation.\n- [hard] REQ-06: Acceptance decisions are specified as exact binomial tests — one-sided against the composite null $\\Pr \\ge 1/q$ evaluated at $p = 1/q$, and two-sided against $1 - \\prod_{i=1}^{n}(1 - q^{-i})$ — with normal-approximation critical values recorded as planning approximations only.\n- [hard] REQ-07: Exclusion and failure rules are predeclared: what halts a cell, what quarantines a shard, what may be rerun, how many attempts are permitted, and how a rerun charges the error budget.\n- [hard] REQ-08: Backend selection is specified as a function of correctness, safety and measured throughput alone, and explicitly never of an observed zero fraction.\n- [hard] REQ-09: The $q = 7$, $n = 20$ cell is committed as the first cell resampled under a preregistered $N$, and the by-product reading motivating that choice stays on the record whatever the resample returns.\n- [hard] REQ-10: The validation plan names the exact enumeration anchors — $q = 3$ up to $n = 4$, $q = 5$ and $q = 7$ up to $n = 3$ — and requires sampler and estimator agreement with them before any estimated cell is believed.\n- [hard] REQ-11: The $q = 3$ arm is framed as reproduction and precision against published numerics, with no wording implying the campaign extends the $q = 3$ curve in $n$.\n- [hard] REQ-12: The convergence-shape comparison is preregistered as a likelihood comparison on observed counts under their binomial sampling distribution, with the range of $n$ over which candidate families are distinguishable reported rather than assumed.\n- [hard] REQ-13: Novelty statements are conditional on the recorded literature search, naming what was searched, what it returned, and that search's stated limits.\n- [hard] REQ-14: The protocol defines a cell's terminal states — completed only with every manifested shard, exactly its preregistered $N$, and final estimates and verdicts; or halted with its reason while the summary preserves every valid manifested shard completed before the halt as pooled raw counts but no final estimates or verdicts — so a cell that is neither is an incomplete campaign rather than a silent omission.\n\n## Notes\n\nThe protocol is published at the dataset home rather than in an issue-scoped area, since it must outlive the work that produced it and be readable beside the data it governs.\n\nThis document carries forward the campaign-design half of the feasibility study's mandate. The study fixed the attainable envelope and closed with a go recommendation and a campaign design; the design is not a finished artefact until it is stated as a protocol that binds a specific campaign, with its cell universe, sample sizes, error budget and failure rules committed in advance. Delivery of the study itself is credited to the completed study work; this document is the contribution that re-validates its design half against what the campaign will actually run, and revises it where measurement since has moved.\n\n## References\n\nThe protocol cites [GGK2025], [HKS2026], and [Scheinerman2024] through the repository citation registry.", | Algebraic binomial or independent event/frame model; no decoded-bit estimator at `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:1141`. |
| F5-S070 | Workspace sweep | `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:1708` |     "description": "A preregistered comparison between candidate shapes for the finite-size deviation $\\delta(n) = \\Pr[\\mathrm{per}(A) = 0] - 1/q$, computed as a likelihood on the observed counts and published as its own artefact.\n\n## Background\n\nThe campaign's measurable target is the shape of $\\delta(n)$, and two families were named in advance as candidates: geometric decay $\\delta \\sim c\\,q^{-\\alpha n}$ and polynomial decay $\\delta \\sim c\\,n^{-\\beta}$. What a campaign can falsify is a model of $\\delta(n)$; what it cannot falsify is either the asymptotic conjecture or the proved floor.\n\nThe comparison is computed on counts rather than on point estimates. Each cell is a binomial observation with a known sample size, so the likelihood of a candidate shape is available directly; fitting point estimates by weighted least squares and comparing by an information criterion discards that structure and misstates the uncertainty, particularly at cells whose counts are small relative to their sample size.\n\nThe honest limit is stated with the result: two families can be told apart only while $\|\\delta(n)\|$ exceeds a few standard errors at several sizes, and the achievable precision means that range is expected to be roughly $n \\lesssim 14$ to $16$.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n- [aspirational] REQ-01: Candidate families $\\delta(n) \\sim c\\,q^{-\\alpha n}$ and $\\delta(n) \\sim c\\,n^{-\\beta}$ are compared by a likelihood computed on the observed zero counts under their binomial sampling distribution, not by weighted least squares on point estimates.\n- [aspirational] REQ-02: The comparison specification — families, parameterisation, cells included, and comparison statistic — is fixed before the comparison is run against campaign data.\n- [aspirational] REQ-03: The range of $n$ over which $\|\\delta(n)\|$ exceeds a stated multiple of its standard error is reported, and no model preference is claimed outside that range.\n- [aspirational] REQ-04: Parameter estimates carry intervals, and the result states whether each family's interval excludes the other family's behaviour.\n- [aspirational] REQ-05: The outcome is recorded whatever it is, including an outcome in which the data do not distinguish the families.\n- [aspirational] REQ-06: The comparison runs from the finalized dataset alone, is rerunnable from it, and records the campaign id and dataset checksum it used.\n- [aspirational] REQ-07: The result is published as its own document with its own fit tables, and modifies no other published artefact.\n\n## Notes\n\nEvery criterion here is aspirational: the deliverable is a characterisation whose informativeness is set by the precision the campaign achieves, which is bounded by measurement rather than by effort.\n\nThis artefact stands alone. | F5-S071 | Workspace sweep | `dev/active/b8206228-permanent-statistics/breakdown.json:1835` |     "description": "The rare-event estimator built to its preregistered design, with unbiasedness and interval coverage demonstrated against exactly known cases.\n\n## Background\n\nA rare-event estimator's output cannot be checked by looking at it: the events it estimates are ones direct sampling never observes. Its credibility comes entirely from behaving correctly where the answer is known. At $k = 1$ the deficiency probability is exactly $q^{-n}$ at every $n$, and small $(q, n, k)$ are exhaustively enumerable, which gives a ladder of cases with known answers running from trivially small up to the edge of what direct sampling can reach.\n\nCoverage is a separate property from unbiasedness and is demonstrated separately: an estimator can be unbiased while reporting intervals that contain the true value far less often than their nominal level claims, and it is the interval that a reader of the result will rely on.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\n- [aspirational] REQ-01: The estimator implements the preregistered proposal and weighting, and a test asserts the implemented weights match the specification on hand-checked cases.\n- [aspirational] REQ-02: Unbiasedness is demonstrated against cases whose probability is known exactly, with the estimator's mean over repeated independent runs agreeing with the exact value within its own interval.\n- [aspirational] REQ-03: Interval coverage is demonstrated empirically: over repeated runs at a stated nominal level, the fraction of intervals containing the exact value matches the nominal level within its own binomial interval.\n- [aspirational] REQ-04: A deviation from the preregistered design made during implementation is recorded with its reason, and the validation is judged against the design as amended rather than silently against the new behaviour.\n- [aspirational] REQ-05: Draws come from the reserved rare-event stream purpose, so no such draw can enter a published zero-fraction cell.\n- [aspirational] REQ-06: A configuration whose effective sample size falls below the design's usability threshold is reported as unusable rather than as an estimate.\n\n## Notes\n\nEvery criterion here is aspirational.
